([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Demography-Skills/skills/demog-data-and-reproducibility) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **demog-literature-positioning**: Use when positioning a Demography (PAA / Duke University Press) manuscript against the population-science literature so it reads as a contribution of general interest to demographers. Demography readers span fertility, mortality, migration, family, and health, so the paper must engage the demographic literatures they expect. Stakes the contribution; it does not write the lit review. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Demography-Skills/skills/demog-literature-positioning) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **demog-rebuttal**: Use when writing the response to a Demography (PAA / Duke University Press) revise-and-resubmit. Demography review is double-blind and synthesized by a topic-area Deputy Editor, so the response must convert every expert reviewer on demographic substance (rates, identification, methods) without alienating the editor. Structures the response letter; it does not fabricate new results. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Demography-Skills/skills/demog-rebuttal) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **demog-research-design**: Use when defending the research design of a Demography (PAA / Duke University Press) manuscript — choosing among demographic methods (life tables, decomposition, event-history/survival, age-period-cohort, multistate, microsimulation, projections) and, where the question is causal, defending identification. Demography judges each method on its own terms. Strengthens the design; it does not write code. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Demography-Skills/skills/demog-research-design) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **demog-review-process**: Use to understand how Demography (PAA / Duke University Press) evaluates a manuscript — double-blind review, pre-review/desk screening by the Editor or a Deputy Editor, the role of topic-area Deputy Editors, and the grounds for desk rejection (inappropriate fit or unlikely to receive favorable reviews). Sets expectations and shapes the paper to survive review; it does not contact editors. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Demography-Skills/skills/demog-review-process) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **demog-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for Demography (PAA / Duke University Press) via ScholarOne Manuscripts — article-type selection, double-blind preparation, word/abstract/highlights/keywords limits, house-style formatting, the $35 submission fee, and the open-access (Subscribe to Open) model. Final checks; it does not draft content. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/Demography-Skills/skills/demog-submission) · ★1.4k · 3 copies)
- **demog-tables-figures**: Use when building tables and figures for a Demography (PAA / Duke University Press) manuscript so exhibits are self-contained, accessible, and within the journal's limits (Research Articles up to ~8 tables and figures; Research Notes up to 4). - **demog-theory-building**: Use when building the argument of a Demography (PAA / Duke University Press) manuscript into a population-science contribution — whether the work is formal/mathematical demography, an explanatory account of fertility/mortality/migration, or a measurement/decomposition advance. - **demolish**: Hostile critical analysis of written text. Use AFTER a draft exists. Systematically finds every structural weakness, logical flaw, undefined concept, internal contradiction, and problematic implication — one at a time. This is not feedback. It is demolition. ([simonediroma/claude-ghost-writer](https://github.com/simonediroma/claude-ghost-writer/tree/HEAD/skills/demolish))
- **demolish-persona**: Runs a demolition of a chapter from the perspective of a specific reader persona. Use after the standard demolish cycle, or instead of it when you want a specific type of reader's reaction. Available personas: target-reader, hostile-reader, domain-expert, editor, out-of-target. The author can also create custom personas. Each persona finds different problems — run multiple for a full picture. ([simonediroma/claude-ghost-writer](https://github.com/simonediroma/claude-ghost-writer/tree/HEAD/skills/demolish-persona))
- **demolition**: Load when the author invokes demolition on a chapter draft, or when muse offers it before marking a chapter final. The demolition skill is hostile critical analysis applied to fiction: it finds every vulnerability in a chapter — one at a time, never softened, no solutions during demolition — and logs each resolved/deferred/accepted issue in the chapter's Demolition Log. It is the strongest pre-final check; the author may decline it, and declining is recorded. ([FiredMosquito831/my-writing-companion](https://github.com/FiredMosquito831/my-writing-companion/tree/HEAD/skills/demolition) · ★2)

- **deniz-dotnet-general.system-text-json-net11**: Imperative guidance for the System.Text.Json APIs added in .NET 11: the built-in `JsonNamingPolicy.PascalCase` naming policy, and the strongly-typed generic `JsonSerializerOptions.GetTypeInfo` and `JsonSerializerOptions.TryGetTypeInfo` metadata accessors (generic overloads that return a typed `JsonTypeInfo`). USE ONLY when the user is targeting net11.0 or later and needs PascalCase JSON property or dictionary-key names without writing a custom naming policy, a strongly-typed generic `JsonTypeInfo` instead of the non-generic `JsonTypeInfo`, or a no-throw way to probe whether a type's serializa… ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-dotnet-general/skills/deniz-dotnet-general.system-text-json-net11) · ★2)
- **deniz-dotnet-general.target-authoring**: Canonical patterns for writing custom MSBuild targets. USE FOR: diagnosing and fixing custom target authoring anti-patterns; broken SDK target chains across files (e.g., Directory.Build.targets silently redefining SDK targets); targets that replace CompileDependsOn instead of extending it with $(CompileDependsOn); query targets returning stale results from Outputs vs Returns misuse; writing a target's Inputs/Outputs; missing FileWrites registration. Covers DependsOnTargets vs BeforeTargets vs AfterTargets, the Build→CoreBuild three-level pattern, and the $(XxxDependsOn) chain-extension patter… ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-dotnet-general/skills/deniz-dotnet-general.target-authoring) · ★2)
- **deniz-dotnet-general.test-analysis-extensions**: Provides file paths to language-specific reference files for the curated pragmatic test-analysis pair: test-anti-patterns and test-gap-analysis. Do not use directly — those skills load the matching extension when they need framework-specific test markers, assertion APIs, skip annotations, sleep patterns, mystery-guest indicators, integration markers, and setup/teardown conventions. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-dotnet-general/skills/deniz-dotnet-general.test-analysis-extensions) · ★2)
- **deniz-dotnet-general.test-anti-patterns**: Audits an existing test file or suite in any language for pragmatic anti-patterns and quality issues, producing a severity-ranked report. Use when asked to review tests, find false-confidence assertions, investigate flakiness or order dependence, identify duplication or magic values, or judge whether assertions repeatedly verify only one facet of behavior. DO NOT USE FOR: writing or running tests, coverage/CRAP metrics, behavioral gap analysis, full assertion-diversity metrics, an academic smell catalog, or fixing the tests it audits (report-only). ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-dotnet-general/skills/deniz-dotnet-general.test-anti-patterns) · ★2)
- **deniz-dotnet-general.test-gap-analysis**: Performs report-only pseudo-mutation analysis of existing tests: find caller-visible production-code changes that current assertions would not catch. Use when asked whether a bug or mutation could survive, where tests are behaviorally blind, or which production outcomes lack protection. Polyglot. DO NOT USE FOR: writing tests, proof that tests catch an identified fix or feature (test-value-evidence), suite organization or taxonomy, coverage/CRAP interpretation, assertion/smell audits, or running a mutation-testing tool. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-dotnet-general/skills/deniz-dotnet-general.test-gap-analysis) · ★2)
- **deniz-dotnet-general.test-value-evidence**: Assess tests for an identified code or test change, feature, PR or commit. USE FOR: review changed behavior or regression tests, prove pre-fix/missing behavior is detected, revision-bound red-green evidence. Polyglot. DO NOT USE without an identified change: hypothetical mutations or general suite blind spots (test-gap-analysis). Also exclude per-test readiness/A-F grades (grade-tests), coverage (coverage-analysis), new suites (code-testing), or mutation engines. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-dotnet-general/skills/deniz-dotnet-general.test-value-evidence) · ★2)
- **deniz-dotnet-general.testcontainers-integration-tests**: Write integration tests using TestContainers for .NET with xUnit, without an Aspire AppHost; for Aspire apps use aspire-integration-testing. Covers infrastructure testing with real databases, message queues, and caches in Docker containers instead of mocks. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-dotnet-general/skills/deniz-dotnet-general.testcontainers-integration-tests) · ★2)
- **deniz-dotnet-general.thread-abort-migration**: Guides migration of .NET Framework Thread.Abort usage to cooperative cancellation in modern .NET. USE FOR: modernizing code that calls Thread.Abort, catching ThreadAbortException, replacing Thread.ResetAbort, replacing Thread.Interrupt for thread termination, resolving PlatformNotSupportedException or SYSLIB0006 after retargeting to .NET 6+, migrating ASP.NET Response.End or Response.Redirect(url, true) which internally call Thread.Abort. DO NOT USE FOR: code that only uses Thread.Join, Thread.Sleep, or Thread.Start without any abort, interrupt, or ThreadAbortException usage — these APIs work… ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-dotnet-general/skills/deniz-dotnet-general.thread-abort-migration) · ★2)
- **deniz-dotnet-general.troubleshoot-binlog-mcp**: Diagnose binlog MCP server startup failures. Checks workspace SDK, feeds, and source mapping without bypassing policy. DO NOT USE FOR: errors inside a binlog or capturing binlogs (use msbuild), unrelated MCP servers. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-dotnet-general/skills/deniz-dotnet-general.troubleshoot-binlog-mcp) · ★2)
- **deniz-dotnet-general.vectorization**: Design, implement, optimize, and review SIMD code in .NET. USE FOR: vectorizing scalar loops with TensorPrimitives, Vector64/128/256/512, or platform hardware intrinsics; reviewing existing SIMD code, including the generic Vector type, for contract equivalence, tail handling, memory safety, portability, fallbacks, and measured performance. DO NOT USE FOR: performance work unrelated to SIMD or vectorization. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-dotnet-general/skills/deniz-dotnet-general.vectorization) · ★2)
- **deniz-dotnet-general.writing-tunit-tests**: Write tests in TUnit — the attributes, the async assertion model, data-driven cases, lifecycle hooks, and the parallelism rules. USE FOR writing or fixing a test in a TUnit project, translating a test idea into TUnit syntax, or reviewing TUnit test code for API mistakes. DO NOT USE FOR deciding what to test, judging whether a test is any good, or running tests. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-dotnet-general/skills/deniz-dotnet-general.writing-tunit-tests) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.asd-ste100**: Use when English text must be parsed without a human to resolve ambiguity — tool descriptions, error messages, inter-agent instructions, system prompts, status reports — and misreading has a real cost, or when text reads as dense, hedged, or easy to misparse. Triggers: disambiguate, STE100 rewrite, apply Simplified Technical English, plain-language rewrite, controlled-language rewrite, rewrite so an agent cannot misread this. Not for creative or marketing copy. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.asd-ste100) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.ask-deniz**: Use when unsure which skill or ceremony fits, or when asked which to use, across installed deniz Modules: process workflows, .NET, Aspire, and Akka. Manual ceremonies stay user-started. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.ask-deniz) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.brainstorming**: Use when the user wants to brainstorm or design something, or a build request leaves what or why open; not for a request that already says what to build. Draws out what they want, sizes the work, and agrees it before building. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.brainstorming) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.codebase-design**: Shared vocabulary for designing deep modules. Use when the user wants to design or improve a module's interface, find deepening opportunities, decide where a seam goes, make code more testable or AI-navigable, or when another skill needs the deep-module vocabulary. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.codebase-design) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.diagnosing-superpowers**: Use when a deniz-process session went wrong and your human partner wants to know why — repeated work, ignored plans, stumbles, poor results, a skill that didn't fire, "it took too long", "why is it so expensive", "what is it doing" — or wants to build a bug report for the superpowers maintainers, for the current session or a past one identified by id or path, on any harness. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.diagnosing-superpowers) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.dispatching-parallel-agents**: Use when facing 2+ independent failures or investigations that can be worked on without shared state or sequential dependencies ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.dispatching-parallel-agents) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.domain-modeling**: Build and sharpen a project's domain model. Use when discussing codebase terminology, writing or editing a GLOSSARY.md, or recording or editing an ADR. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.domain-modeling) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.executing-plans**: Use when executing an implementation plan in the current session as the implementer yourself — inline execution was chosen, or no subagent tool is available ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.executing-plans) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.finishing-a-development-branch**: Use when implementation is complete, all tests pass, and you need to decide how to integrate the work ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.finishing-a-development-branch) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.grill-me**: A relentless interview to sharpen a plan or design. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.grill-me) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.grill-with-docs**: A relentless interview to sharpen a plan or design, which also creates docs (ADR's and glossary) as we go. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.grill-with-docs) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.grilling**: Grill the user relentlessly about a plan, decision, or idea. Use when the user wants to stress-test their thinking, or uses any 'grill' trigger phrases. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.grilling) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.handoff**: Compact the current conversation into a handoff document for another agent to pick up. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.handoff) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.implement**: Implement a piece of work based on a spec or set of tickets. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.implement) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.implement-spec**: Build a spec's tickets in parallel on one integration branch. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.implement-spec) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.improve-codebase-architecture**: Scan a codebase for deepening opportunities, present them as a visual HTML report, then grill through whichever one you pick. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.improve-codebase-architecture) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.ponytail**: Use when the user asks for the simplest solution, says "be lazy" or "yagni", names ponytail, or complains about over-engineering or bloat: the smallest change that fully solves the task, and a reply a busy human understands in one read. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.ponytail) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.ponytail-audit**: Quality audit of a whole repo: bugs, security holes, what breaks under real load, risky code without tests, slow paths, and what to delete, merge or split. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.ponytail-audit) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.ponytail-debt**: List every `shortcut:` comment (and older `ponytail:` ones) as a debt ledger. One-shot report, changes nothing. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.ponytail-debt) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.ponytail-review**: Quality review of a change: is the logic right, is it safe, does it hold under real load, is risky code tested, is it fast enough, and is every line needed. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.ponytail-review) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.pr**: Use when writing a PR body. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.pr) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.prototype**: Build a throwaway prototype to answer a design question. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.prototype) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.receiving-code-review**: Use when receiving code review feedback, before implementing suggestions, especially if feedback seems unclear or technically questionable - requires technical rigor and verification, not performative agreement or blind implementation ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.receiving-code-review) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.requesting-code-review**: Use after each task in subagent-driven development, after completing a major feature, before merging to main, or when asked to review changes since a commit, branch or tag — pins the range, then reports Standards and Spec separately ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.requesting-code-review) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.research**: Investigate a question against high-trust primary sources and capture the findings as a Markdown file in the repo. Use when the user wants a topic researched, docs or API facts gathered, or reading legwork delegated to a background agent. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.research) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.retro**: Conduct a retrospective on a coding session. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.retro) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.setup-matt-pocock-skills**: Configure this repo for the engineering skills: set up its issue tracker, triage label vocabulary, and domain doc layout. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.setup-matt-pocock-skills) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.software-factory**: Shared language for designing a software factory: the tasks it runs and the services it plugs into. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.software-factory) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.subagent-driven-development**: Use when executing implementation plans with independent tasks in the current session ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.subagent-driven-development) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.systematic-debugging**: Use when encountering any bug, test failure, performance regression, or unexpected behavior, before proposing fixes ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.systematic-debugging) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.teach**: Teach the user a new skill or concept, within this workspace. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.teach) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.test-driven-development**: Use when implementing any feature or bugfix, before writing implementation code — the red-green loop, the seam to test at, and one slice at a time ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.test-driven-development) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.to-questionnaire**: Turn a decision you can't fully answer into a questionnaire for someone else to fill in. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.to-questionnaire) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.to-spec**: Turn the current conversation into a spec and publish it to the project issue tracker: no interview, just synthesis of what you've already discussed. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.to-spec) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.to-tickets**: Break a plan, spec, or the current conversation into a set of tracer-bullet tickets, each declaring its blocking edges, published to the configured tracker (edges as text in one file per ticket locally, or native blocking links on a real tracker). ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.to-tickets) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.triage**: Move issues and external PRs through a state machine of triage roles, categorise, verify, grill if needed, and write agent-ready briefs. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.triage) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.using-git-worktrees**: Use when work needs isolation from the current workspace, because the user asked for it or a concrete conflict requires it - ensures an isolated workspace exists via native tools or git worktree fallback ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.using-git-worktrees) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.using-superpowers**: Switch this session to skill-driven working: check for a relevant skill before each task, process skills first. - **deniz-process.verification-before-completion**: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.verification-before-completion) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.wait-what**: Stop. That last message did not land: re-pitch it. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.wait-what) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.wayfinder**: Plan a huge chunk of work (more than one agent session can hold) as a shared map of decision tickets on your issue tracker, and resolve them one at a time until the way to the destination is clear. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.wayfinder) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.wizard**: Generate an interactive bash wizard that walks a human through steps only they can perform. Use when provisioning infrastructure, setting up credentials or CI secrets, walking an unfamiliar third-party dashboard, or running a one-off migration or cutover. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.wizard) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.writing-for-agents**: Writing documents an agent consumes. ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.writing-for-agents) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.writing-plans**: Use when you have a spec or requirements for multi-session work, or several people's worth, before touching code — not for a change that fits one sitting ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.writing-plans) · ★2)
- **deniz-process.writing-skills**: Use when creating new skills, editing existing skills, or verifying skills work before deployment ([Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins](https://github.com/Blind-Striker/agent-skills-and-plugins/tree/HEAD/opencode/deniz-process/skills/deniz-process.writing-skills) · ★2)
